# Isolated primary judgment contract

Read the execution plan and entire lossless reader, including every document and shared block, in bounded untruncated outputs. Only plan-allowed evidence may be read. Follow exact model and effort in the plan. No peer outputs, other debates, historical scores, publication prose, rankings, biographies, browsing, session logs, credentials or environment inspection. No paid calls, external inference or subagents. Isolation is procedural, not a filesystem sandbox. Confirm the absolute output path; tell the controller when reading is complete and wait for authenticated reading release before authoring.

## Evidence and judgment

The frozen motion is “What is the best account of objective morality?”. Adam Lloyd Johnson is pro (God grounds objective morality); Erik Wielenberg is con (Objective morality needs no divine foundation). Both accept objective morality. Assess only the approved direct debater exchange, approximately 33:05–1:13:25, using the exact source bounds and ownership map in source notes. Entire introductory moderator-led discussion, audience questions, all dependent answers and Johnson's final audience-dependent response are excluded. Give no credit, penalty, concession, reply link or absence inference from excluded material. No formal opening speeches or paired closings exist. Earlier statements referred to in the retained exchange count only insofar as independently stated there; do not import their excluded supporting arguments. Keep questions as challenges, preserve distinctions between moral value, obligation and punishment, and distinguish origins of moral beliefs from the truth or ground of moral facts when the source does. Do not require Wielenberg to disprove all gods or either speaker to defeat moral nihilism. Do not infer a theory change from caption-corrupted “communitarian,” identify the corrupted animal example, or score the ambiguous transition clause enumerated in source notes. Mixed handoffs are excluded from selected evidence. Audio retrieval failed before transcription, so no independent audio verification or direct listening occurred. Recognition uncertainty cannot become a speaker's weakness; genuine limitations of the argument actually given remain assessable under the rubric.

Evaluate every locked move exactly once, in inventory order, under the complete rubric. Preserve all inventory fields. Never calculate move, section or overall totals or winners. Each dimension rationale must identify its actual source-specific strength or limitation.

## Exact output schema

A single JSON object with schemaVersion "1.0-standalone-primary-judgment", protocolId "assessment-production-standalone-debate-v1", status "complete-and-schema-valid", pass from plan, debateNumber as string from plan, debateId from plan, reviewerRole "isolated-score-blind-primary-judge", assessmentModel "5.6 Sol", reasoningEffort "low", inventorySha256 from judgment packet, isolation, judgments, burdenCompletionAdjustment, audit.

isolation contains booleans legacyAssessmentsUnavailable, calculatedTotalsUnavailable, winnerLabelsUnavailable, otherJudgmentUnavailable, publicationProseUnavailable, otherDebatesUnavailable (all true only if accurate), and contaminationDetected (false only if accurate). Report a breach and stop if any required isolation condition is false.

judgments is an array exactly matching inventory moves. Each entry: {moveId,assessmentConfidence,dimensions}. assessmentConfidence is high, medium or low. dimensions has exactly logicalCoherence, evidenceWarrant, responsiveness, relevanceBurden, precisionClarity, calibrationCharity. Each dimension has exactly {value,rationale}; value is integer 0–100, rationale at least 40 characters. Use rubric anchors; do not give duplicate deductions for one defect unless it has distinct demonstrated consequences in multiple dimensions.

burdenCompletionAdjustment has pro and con. Each is exactly {value,rationale,eligibility}, with integer value −5..5. eligibility has exactly these nine keys: distinctDebateWideConsequence (boolean), affectsBurdenCompletion (boolean), notAlreadyScored (boolean), affectedBurdenIds (array of actual route bridge IDs), completionCriterion (string), relatedMoveIds (array of actual move IDs), distinctConsequence (string), alreadyCapturedBy (array), counterfactual (string). Duplicate capture (nonempty alreadyCapturedBy or false notAlreadyScored) requires zero. Nonzero requires all three booleans true, nonempty affectedBurdenIds and relatedMoveIds, empty alreadyCapturedBy, and completionCriterion, distinctConsequence, counterfactual each at least 30 characters. A value of zero still requires every eligibility key. Explain the actual debate-wide question and why any claimed adjustment does not rescore a move. Never calculate totals to choose an adjustment.

audit has completeLockedInventoryReviewed, allMovesJudgedOnce, ratingsOnlyNoCalculatedScores, publicationBlind, scoreBlind, all true only if accurate.

## One unsaved review and exact submission

After authenticated reading release, create the complete candidate in memory. Run the pinned checker with standard input; do not inspect its implementation or create a disk draft. Review every rationale yourself against source ownership, recognition restrictions, adopted burdens and later qualifications before holding it. Send exact UTF-8 JSON bytes from your existing memory to the pinned `standalone-workflow.mjs handoff hold` command with the explicit debate and your plan. The checker validates and the holder keeps bytes in memory. Keep the holder alive and send its receipt. The controller reads all candidate bytes and issues source-compliance release; it may not negotiate ratings or disclose the peer pass. Release writes the approved bytes exactly once. Stop authoring after submission. One context, one submission, no automatic retries or overwrites. Ordinary corrections before a successful hold are within this context; an existing hold or saved failure is never silently replaced.

DOCUMENT docs/reassessment-rubric-v2.1.md
# Slugfester Reassessment Rubric v2.1

Score the argumentative performance present in the locked source—not whether a worldview is true, admirable, popular, or familiar. A score is earned move by move and has no automatic floor.

## Shared band anchors

| Band | Operational meaning |
| --- | --- |
| 95–100 | Exceptional and unusually complete: explicit inferential structure, strong public warrant, direct engagement with the strongest live reply, and little material repair needed. |
| 85–94 | Very strong: the move substantially advances its burden and survives the best live objection, with only bounded residual gaps. |
| 75–84 | Strong/competent: coherent and relevant, but compressed, incompletely warranted, or vulnerable at one important bridge. |
| 65–74 | Mixed: a useful core is present, yet a material premise, inference, or reply remains underdeveloped. |
| 50–64 | Weak: a major burden is not discharged, or the move largely misses the live issue despite some relevant content. |
| 25–49 | Very weak: the inference is seriously defective, substantially unsupported, or mostly unresponsive. |
| 0–24 | Non-performance: no usable argument on the assigned issue, a self-defeating contradiction, or content that is essentially irrelevant to the burden. |

Use 50, 70, 80, and 90 as decision boundaries, not default magnets. Before moving a dimension by five points, identify the particular transcript evidence or missing bridge that warrants the change.

## Dimensions

### Logical coherence — 25%

- **90+**: premises, intermediate steps, scope, and conclusion are explicit and mutually consistent; the strongest live validity challenge is answered.
- **75–89**: the inference is intelligible and mostly valid but leaves one bridge implicit or one ambiguity unresolved.
- **50–74**: the conclusion depends on a material equivocation, unsupported conditional, false dilemma, internal tension, or non sequitur.
- **Below 50**: the move is self-undermining, plainly invalid at its core, or lacks an inferential route to its conclusion.

Do not lower this dimension merely because a premise lacks evidence; that belongs primarily under evidence/warrant.

### Evidence and warrant — 20%

- **90+**: relevant evidence is specific, sourceable, representative, and connected to the conclusion through defended warrants and serious alternative comparison.
- **75–89**: appropriate support is offered, but documentation, independence, base rates, rival hypotheses, or a key warrant remains compressed.
- **50–74**: authority, anecdote, assertion, selective examples, or speculative mechanisms carry substantial weight without adequate checking.
- **Below 50**: a fact-dependent conclusion is effectively unsupported or relies on evidence contradicted within the locked record.

Lack of an inline citation is not itself disproof. Score the degree to which verification matters to the inference.

### Responsiveness — 20%

- **90+**: accurately identifies and answers the opponent's strongest relevant claim at its decisive premise or inference.
- **75–89**: directly engages the live issue but leaves a major component unanswered or answers it only conditionally.
- **50–74**: responds to a secondary point, a weaker reconstruction, or only one part of a compound objection.
- **Below 50**: evades, changes the subject, or attacks a position the opponent did not advance.

Constructive openings are scored for responsiveness to the motion and known burden, not penalized for lacking an earlier opponent move.

### Relevance and burden — 15%

- **90+**: directly discharges a locked burden or decisively defeats an opponent burden, with a conclusion proportionate to what that success establishes.
- **75–89**: materially advances the burden but does not complete it or leaves one required link unstated.
- **50–74**: related to the topic but peripheral, burden-shifting, or stronger in rhetoric than in motion-level consequence.
- **Below 50**: irrelevant to the locked motion or depends on assigning the opponent a burden they did not adopt.

Judge opportunity fairly. Do not invent a positive contrary burden for a side whose declared role is critical.

### Precision and clarity — 10%

- **90+**: key terms, modality, comparison class, scope, and confidence are stable and easy to audit.
- **75–89**: the move is understandable with only minor ambiguity or compression.
- **50–74**: unstable terminology, vague quantifiers, unclear referents, or missing distinctions materially obscure evaluation.
- **Below 50**: the claim cannot be reconstructed reliably enough to assess.

Fluency, wit, and polish are not clarity unless they improve the recoverable argument.

### Calibration and charity — 10%

- **90+**: confidence matches the evidence, concessions are accurate, alternatives are represented in their strongest live form, and uncertainty is explicit.
- **75–89**: generally proportionate and fair, with limited overstatement or incomplete treatment of an alternative.
- **50–74**: significant overclaiming, selective skepticism, motive attribution, or an avoidably weak representation of the alternative.
- **Below 50**: certainty is radically disproportionate or the move depends on a gross caricature.

Do not award charity merely for politeness or deduct it for an abrasive tone unless the tone changes the argumentative representation.

## Penalty controls

- Identify one primary home for each defect. Apply effects in additional dimensions only when the transcript shows genuinely distinct consequences.
- Compatibility is not explanation; possibility can answer contradiction without establishing probability.
- Familiar named arguments receive no pedigree bonus. Score only the version actually presented.
- A fallacy or bias tag never creates an extra deduction.
- Audience response, status, accent, speed, and confidence are excluded.
- Missing replies are represented by response links and burden completion; do not manufacture paired moves.
- Score diagnostic questions as questions or challenges, not as unargued contrary theories.

## Aggregation

Move scores use fixed dimension weights. Section scores are rounded importance-weighted means of selected moves. Overall scores are prelocked section-weighted means plus a −5 to +5 burden-completion adjustment. No additional coverage, coherence, resilience, or global-calibration meta-score is added; those qualities are already visible in the moves and burden record.

See [`assessment-workflow-v2.1.md`](assessment-workflow-v2.1.md) for formulas, disagreement thresholds, provenance, and promotion gates.

END DOCUMENT

# Frozen source restrictions — Debate 289

The canonical source is the complete supplied 2020 Wielenberg–Johnson recording, video bg2WK-IIrd8. This is not the 2018 Craig–Wielenberg event. The publisher dates it September 27, 2020, University of Nebraska-Lincoln. The motion is byte-identically: What is the best account of objective morality?

Pro: Adam Lloyd Johnson — God grounds objective morality. Con: Erik Wielenberg — Objective morality needs no divine foundation. Both accept objective morality. Assess their comparative explanations and actual challenges; do not require either to prove objective morality against a nihilist, or require Wielenberg to disprove every God merely because he offers a non-theistic moral account.

## Approved scope

This assessment covers only the debaters’ direct exchange (approximately 33:05–1:13:25), as approved by the editor. The introductory position discussion, audience questions and all dependent answers, including the final reply to an audience question, are excluded. This recording has no separate formal opening speeches or paired closing statements. Neither speaker receives credit, penalties, or an inference of failing to reply from excluded material. These scores assess the direct exchange, not the complete recording.

The chapter starts at about 33:05; Wielenberg’s first substantive direct question begins at 33:22 (canonical index 916, human label E0917). Retained argument ends at 1:13:25.840 (canonical index 2006, E2007). Index 2007 is “My time is up,” and index 2008 starts the audience transition. Exclude all earlier and later source from ratings, quotations, responses, burdens and absence inferences. Earlier propositions restated independently in the retained exchange may be assessed only in that retained form; do not import excluded reasons. A shared assumption can identify the debate’s issue without receiving evidential credit.

Read the full source to establish boundaries. Moderator interjections only assign turns or time. No substantive host argument belongs to a scored side. No opening or closing should be invented from this dialogue. Do not treat Johnson’s final answer after the audience animal-value question as a formal closing.

## Event indexing and ownership

The indexed transcript uses human labels E0001 through E3103. The events array and every startEvent/endEvent field are ZERO-BASED: E0001 corresponds to index 0; subtract one from each human label. Millisecond endpoints come from events.json exactly. Caption display durations overlap turn transitions; they do not establish overlapping speech. Choose the smallest complete supporting text and avoid mixed handoff events.

Direct-exchange ownership (human E labels, inclusive):
- Wielenberg question E0917–E0977; Johnson answer E0978–E1055.
- Johnson question E1058–E1072; Wielenberg answer E1073–E1184.
- Wielenberg question E1188–E1235; exclude mixed E1236; Johnson answer E1237–E1321.
- Johnson question E1324–E1376; Wielenberg answer E1377–E1436.
- Wielenberg question E1440–E1467; Johnson answer E1468–E1549.
- Johnson question E1552–E1606, with its last summary clause spilling into mixed E1607; exclude E1607. Earlier full supporting challenge remains available. Wielenberg answer E1608–E1712.
- Wielenberg question E1716–E1738; Johnson answer E1739–E1825.
- Johnson question E1829–E1898; Wielenberg answer E1899–E2007.
Other material inside the window is logistical. Do not claim finer audio verification than available.

## Automatic-caption limitations

Only one English track exists; no alternative English transcript was available. Initial public audio byte probe returned 206, but full public retrieval returned 403, so no audio was acquired and no paid transcription occurred. The seven planned clip checks were NOT completed. No direct audio listening is claimed. Every selected below-high-attribution move still needs successful required verification before adjudication; never label uncertainty high merely to avoid that requirement.

Do not derive a rating or quotation from: E1718’s uncertain transition polarity; E1748’s “communitarian” label (the theory is repeatedly called Trinitarian elsewhere); E1894’s corrupt first animal name (“beanies”); mixed handoffs E1236 and E1607. Do not silently correct them. Clear surrounding arguments may be assessed without these terms. Outside-scope caption names such as “William Lane Craig” addressing Wielenberg are recognition errors, not another participant or scored error. General disfluency or caption syntax is not evidence of poor reasoning.

## Complete featured quotations

Every eligible quote must be an exact, self-contained complete thought, 3–18 words, retaining any qualification necessary to represent the speaker fairly. Do not truncate a concession before a challenge. Examples of complete clear candidates within the assessed source are Johnson’s “the ultimate good is what God is like.” (E1055), and Wielenberg’s “a mother's love for her child is intrinsically good.” (E1623–E1624). The inventory author must verify any chosen quote in its selected exact source span and surrounding context before freeze; these examples do not prescribe move selection or ratings.
